## Context

- T0 writes feedback; T1, T2 and both AOT kinds read it as a FactSnapshot. Baseline-AOT ICs write the same schema (H33). PGO profiles (`.ep9prof`) and TS hints (HINTED) use it too, so the three fact sources are interchangeable inputs (H30).
- Depends on: H2 (slot kinds are ABI bytes), H8 (ShapeIds are run-local; IC keys hold weak shape pointers), H12 (no address identity), H13 (stable indices), H17 (shapes), H18 (elements kinds).
- Depended on by: H29 (profiling), H30 (fact lattice), H33 (AOT feedback rule), H35 (unit header `fact_schema`), H37 (HINTED entries).
- Goals: G2-G4 (one-compare IC guards), G11 (dual-mode contracts from M0).

## Decision

Copied from the plan row: FeedbackVector after 8 calls. v1 slot kinds. CacheIR-like guard/action lists; mono → ≤ 4-way poly → megamorphic stub cache. IC state only in RW data; no code patching in JIT or AOT; optional embedded stub corpus. PGO profiles, hints and Warp-style snapshots share the schema; baseline-AOT ICs write it.

The week-4 decisions below fill the parts the plan defers (kind **fill**; decisions-log §11).

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| K1 | **Slot kinds v0** = the 21 existing `FeedbackSlotKind` bytes 0-20 and names. No ABI bump. `RuntimeId` stays a placeholder (19 ids then; 48 ids, 0-47, since ABI 5: H02-Q5) | Each slot operand maps to one kind. The ABI fingerprint hashes names and bytes, not docs; a rename re-blesses 88 goldens and the corpus digest for no gain |
| K2 | **Runtime record**: one 16-byte entry `[key \| value]` per slot. The key is weak, the value strong. A new entry is `[undefined \| int32 0]` | One entry format for T0, JIT and AOT RW cells (OM-5, H8) |
| K3 | **IC states** (OM-6): key undefined = uninitialized; a shape cell = monomorphic; the hole with a POLY_IC cell (≤ 4 cases) = polymorphic; the hole with an int32 = megamorphic, sticky | V8's polymorphism limit is 4 |
| K4 | **Handlers v0** (OM-7): int32 handlers for own data load and store; IC_HANDLER cells for prototype load, missing property and add-property; array and string length by kind; accessor and dictionary receivers record the state and take the slow path | Covers richards and deltablue (M0 exit 4) |
| K5 | **Invalidation v0** (OM-8): one per-isolate `proto_epoch`, bumped on any structural change of a prototype; prototype-chain handlers store and compare it. M1 replaces it with validity cells. **Built 2026-10-26 (M4-P1 VALIDITY; plan v9 V9-D7, IC_LAYOUT 2): validity cells replace the epoch.** One VALIDITY_CELL (kind 79, 48 B: +8 int32 state (1 valid, 0 invalid), +16 its users list (a weak array of the TRANSITIONS kind), +24 int32 users in the list, +32 int32 depth, +40 int32 the epoch when made) per prototype, in its PROTO_INFO +32 (`crates/ep9_vm/src/validity.rs`); the null prototype's cell (depth 0) never becomes invalid. A cell is made at a recording miss (`validity::cell_for`), only under a valid cell of the next prototype, whose users list it joins; a structural change of a prototype (`shape::note_change`, OM-8) clears its cell and every user cell, recursively, and stops at a cell that is already invalid (it has no valid user). A `PROTO_LOAD`, `MISSING` or 0x24 `ADD` handler keeps the cell of the receiver shape's prototype at +16 (strong; a cell refers to no object, so K7's aim holds), and its hit loads the cell and compares one word. A change of a prototype off the receiver's chain no longer makes a case miss, and nothing counts, so nothing saturates (K18). `GLOBAL` (keyed on two shapes, no chain) and a 0x25 `ADD` keep no cell. A chain deeper than 64 prototypes gets no cell, and its cases are not recorded. The case computation stays no GC point (K14): it reads the cell it sees; if that cell is not valid at `ic::record`, the record makes the current one and walks the chain again, and records the case only if the answer holds. `proto_epoch` still counts (the `Cx` field is frozen until M5-P0, plan v9 V9-A25) and feeds only a debug cross-check that walks the chain at a hit (plan v9 R1). [Revised 2026-10-26, M4-P1 review R-K5-1: the cross-check walks the chain only when some prototype changed since the cell was made (the epoch is saturated or differs from the one the cell keeps, `ic.rs` `cross_check`), so it checks how a change spreads over the users lists, not a missing `note_change` call: a structural change that never calls `note_change` moves neither the epoch nor a cell, and the cross-check skips that hit.] | Correct, about 3 instructions on a hit. M4-P1: one epoch for the isolate made every prototype case miss after any prototype change and saturated (K18); a cell per prototype misses only for the changed chain |
| K6 | **Vector** (OM-12, OM-13): FEEDBACK cell (kind 68) per function, owned by a per-unit strong root range; made on the 8th call or the first JumpLoop budget poll; `--feedback-threshold` 0 = eager, u32::MAX = never; fp-3 keeps new.target | V8 allocates after 8 calls; eager mode lets test262 run the ICs |
| K7 | **GC** (OM-14, plan rule 8): in IC and feedback data every heap reference is weak, except the value half of a feedback entry and an IC_HANDLER's own cell | IC data must not keep shapes or prototypes alive |
| K8 | **FactSnapshot v0** = `ep9_facts`, `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` 1 (below). **2026-11-02 (M6-P0 SCHEMA6): `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` 2**, the one month-6 bump (V11-D10; the 2026-11-02 rewrite (version) row of the Change log): record flags and the confidence byte, the shape's elements kind and field hints, the keyed hint and kinds set, the elements kinds in the VM order, and four open lists. The block below is the version 1 summary; the full text is `crates/ep9_facts/tests/golden/schema.txt` | Position-independent facts for `.ep9prof` and AOT (H29, H30, H33) |

Per kind (byte, opcodes, runtime record, fact record). "IC" is the K3 entry; "bits" is an int32 bitset in the value word. **Note 2026-10-26 (M4-P0, IC_LAYOUT 2):** the "Runtime record" column is the IC_LAYOUT 1 record. From IC_LAYOUT 2 on, the runtime record of each slot kind is the slot table in the `crates/ep9_vm/src/profile.rs` module docs; the 2026-10-26 rewrite (version) row of the Change log lists the kinds whose record changes (0 at `Construct*` sites, 2, 4, 6, 10, 14, 16, 17, 18, 20).

| Byte | Kind | Opcodes | Runtime record | Fact record |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Call | 0x5e-0x67, 0x69-0x6b, 0x8f call | callee (weak) or hole / int32 count. **M4-P1 (H16-Q2):** at `Call*` sites the key is the native word of the callee's FunctionInfo (FUNCTION +24; not a cell, so no barrier) or the hole, and the count holds only the sampled calls (the first 64 budget units after each poll, `CALL_WINDOW`); `Construct*` sites keep the constructor (weak) and a `CONSTRUCT_SITE` handler or an int32 count (`construct_ic.rs`) | CallFacts: count, IC state, ≤ 4 FunctionRef, result TypeSet |
| 1 | LoadNamed | 0x21, 0x22, 0x8f load | IC | PropertyFacts: state, ≤ 4 (ShapeDesc, Access), value TypeSet |
| 2, 4, 6 | LoadKeyed, StoreKeyed, DefineKeyedOwn | 0x23; 0x26; 0x27, 0x29 | IC | KeyedFacts: PropertyFacts + KeySet |
| 3, 5 | StoreNamed, DefineNamedOwn | 0x24; 0x25 | IC | PropertyFacts |
| 7, 8, 9 | LoadGlobal, LoadGlobalInsideTypeof, StoreGlobal | 0x10; 0x11; 0x12 | holder shape (weak) / handler | GlobalFacts: BindingSet, value TypeSet |
| 10 | HasKeyed | 0x53 | IC | KeyedFacts (OwnField, ProtoField or Missing) |
| 11 | BinaryOp | 0x2a-0x45, 0x59, 0x5a | - / BinaryHint bits | BinaryHint |
| 12 | CompareOp | 0x4b-0x50 | - / CompareHint bits | CompareHint |
| 13 | TypeOf | 0x48 | - / TypeSet bits | TypeSet |
| 14 | InstanceOf | 0x52 | constructor (weak) / int32 state | TargetFacts: state, ≤ 4 FunctionRef |
| 15 | Literal | 0x6c, 0x6d, 0x6e, 0x70, 0x73 | last shape (weak) / int32 count | LiteralFacts: count, ShapeDesc, ElementsKind |
| 16 | CloneObject | 0x72 | source shape (weak) / handler | CloneFacts: state, ≤ 4 (source, result) ShapeDesc |
| 17 | StoreInArrayLiteral | 0x28 | IC | KeyedFacts (Element cases) |
| 18 | ForIn | 0x8c, 0x8d | - / ForInHint bits | ForInHint |
| 19 | JumpLoop | 0x7d | - / int32 back-edge count. **M4-P1 (H16-Q2):** sampled: only a back edge that polls (it used up the interrupt budget) counts | LoopFacts |
| 20 | CreateClosure | 0x74 | the one closure (weak) / int32 0, 1, 2 | ClosureFacts: none, one, many |

T0 v0 writes only the IC entries of 0x21, 0x22, 0x24, 0x25 and the global sites 0x10-0x12 (P3-IC). **M4-P1:** T0 also writes BinaryOp, CompareOp, TypeOf, the keyed kinds, StoreInArrayLiteral, Call at `Call*` sites, JumpLoop, ForIn and CreateClosure (`profile.rs`, with the H16-Q2 cost rules), and Literal, CloneObject and the Call slot of `Construct*` sites (`literal_site.rs`, `construct_ic.rs`, OBJ-ALLOC); InstanceOf stays empty until IC2 (M4-P3). 0x8f `GetIterator` keeps its load slot empty (I34).

**IC v0 as built (P3-IC, week 4).** The layouts and the handler table are in the module docs of `crates/ep9_vm/src/ic.rs`.

| # | As built | Why |
|---|---|---|
| K9 | **Hit test**: `(key as u32) == word0.shape_id` of the receiver (the cage is aligned, so a shape's low 32 bits are its ShapeId), then the handler. An int32 handler has its kind in bits 0-3 and a byte offset in bits 4-31: `FIELD_IN_OBJECT`, `FIELD_OUT_OF_LINE`, `ARRAY_LENGTH`, `STRING_LENGTH`, `SLOW`, `MEGA`. **Appended 2026-10-29 (M5-P2 CONSTREP; Append-only below):** 9-14 `FIELD_INT32_*`, `FIELD_NUMBER_*`, `FIELD_HEAP_*` (`_IN_OBJECT` odd, `_OUT_OF_LINE` even; `crates/ep9_vm/src/ic.rs` `smi`), a field store case (0x24, 0x25) of a field whose representation is not ANY (H17 rep/const row): the hit stores only a value whose tag fits the representation, else it misses. Store sites only; `smi::plain` gives the field kind that readers outside the hit path see | One compare and one load for an own field; no shape load (I44) [2026-11-04, M6-P3 B1W; FREEZE-M1 B1; M6-PLAN-D9, MODES-CHECK MC-w23-10, W20C-7:] **The width rule.** The kind field is 4 bits (`smi::KIND_MASK` 0xF; the byte offset is bits 4-31). Used: 1-6 and 9-14. Free: 7, 8 and 15; 0 is never assigned (a new entry's value is int32 0, K2). A checked field kind is a pair, in-object odd and out of line the next even value, whose representation is `rep_of_kind(k) = (k - 7) >> 1`. One new checked representation (a value 5-7 of H17 "representation values (append-only)") fits in kinds 7 and 8, with a new mapping (`rep_of_kind` gives 0 there, NONE, which no store checks). A second one needs the pair (15, 16), and 16 does not fit: it needs a wider kind field, an IC_LAYOUT change. Any other new handler kind, once the smi kinds are used up, is an IC_HANDLER cell kind (the int32 at +8, K10; 1-11 today), at the cost of one more load, never a wrapped value. 15 stays the probe value at the top of the range. Check: `tests::layout3::ic_handler_kind_lists_state_their_width` (the 4-bit field, the assigned kinds, 0 and 15 no checked kind (15 is the top-of-range probe, README §3.4; 7 and 8 are not probed), and the next pair (15, 16) does not fit). No code change (M6-PLAN-D9). |
| K10 | **Cells**: POLY_IC (69, 72 B) = 4 cases (key weak, handler strong). IC_HANDLER (70, 40 B) = +8 kind, +16 epoch, +24 holder or target shape (weak), +32 location (an int32 field handler). Handler kinds: `PROTO_LOAD`, `MISSING`, `ADD`, `GLOBAL`. **M4-P1 (2026-10-26, K5):** +16 is the validity cell (`PROTO_LOAD`, `MISSING`, 0x24 `ADD`; traced strongly), undefined (0x25 `ADD`) or int32 0 (`GLOBAL`) | K7: only handlers are strong (and, from M4-P1, the validity cells they keep, which refer to no object) |
| K11 | **Strings** have ShapeId 0: their case key is `KEY_STRING` (int32 0), and `STRING_LENGTH` checks the kind byte (symbols have ShapeId 0 too; [2026-11-05, spec-temporal B3] BigInts (kind 9) have ShapeId 0 too, and `ic.rs` `case_key` makes no case for a symbol or a BigInt receiver, so it always misses). Only `length` is cached on strings. **M4-P3 (IC2, 2026-10-27):** a string's method load (`s.charCodeAt`) is cached too, as a `PROTO_LOAD` or `MISSING` case keyed on `KEY_STRING` whose chain starts at %String.prototype% (with that chain's validity cell); its hit checks the kind byte, so a symbol misses (`crates/ep9_vm/src/ic.rs:499-502`). Such a case never enters the stub cache (H16-Q3). No IC_LAYOUT bump: the pairing of `KEY_STRING` with these handler kinds uses the IC_HANDLER layout that IC_LAYOUT 2 declared, and no persisted reader has it (orchestrator decision P3-ORCH-8). [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P3 integration fixer: "Only `length` is cached on strings" held until IC2 3d7632b] | No shape for strings in v0 |
| K12 | **`SLOW` cases** keep their place and take the generic path with no epoch: dictionary shapes (shared, so they cannot tell own keys), accessors, read-only stores, and the exotic keys of `exotic.rs` (a String wrapper's `length`; an array's `length` as a store target; a function's `prototype`, except a load of one already stored). Array-index keys are never cached | A site that sees such objects does not churn; mega stays for >4 shapes |
| K13 | **Add cases** are checked after the store ran: the new shape must be the receiver shape's child with the key as the next {w, e, c} data field. Prototype-mode (unique) shapes get no add case; a FUNCTION shape without a stored `prototype` gets `SLOW`. An out-of-line add hits only if the SLOTS cell has room (growth is slow). 0x24 compares the epoch; 0x25 does not (CreateDataProperty ignores the chain). A non-extensible object has a dictionary shape (OM-10), so an add case never matches it | One shape compare guards the whole transition |
| K14 | **Miss path**: the case is computed before the generic operation (no GC point, no JS) and recorded only if the operation succeeded (a throw records nothing) | A failed or reentrant operation cannot leave a wrong case |
| K15 | **Global ICs** (stretch, built): a lexical binding is a field case keyed on the lexical record's shape (the TDZ hole misses); an own data property of the global object is a `GLOBAL` cell keyed on the global object's shape whose +24 is the lexical record's shape (weak), so a new `let` that shadows it misses. A found global costs one table scan on the miss path (W4-P1-GLOBAL-ONE-SCAN) | Without it, each global load scans the lexical record and the global object |
| K16 | **Shared slots**: `ic::init_vector` scans the function's bytecode when the vector is made; an IC slot that sites with different names share starts megamorphic. BytecodeGen never shares one; hand-built units (tests) do | One name's handler must never answer another name |
| K17 | **0x22** is keyed on the lookup start (the home object's prototype) and passes its `receiver` register as the receiver (W4-P3-SUPER-SPLIT) | §13.3.7.3 |
| K18 | **The epoch saturates** at `u32::MAX` (W4-P3FIX-EPOCH-SAT). There a miss records `SLOW` for a prototype load, a missing property or a 0x24 add, so every older handler misses and only the generic path runs from then on. The real fix is K5: validity cells in M1. **M4-P1 (2026-10-26):** no case compares the epoch since K5's validity cells; the epoch still saturates, but a miss under it records the real case, so a saturated epoch changes no IC (`tests::ic::a_saturated_epoch_changes_no_ic_result`, `tests::validity::two_to_the_32_changes_saturate_nothing`) | A wrap brought back an old handler's epoch and its stale answer after 2^32 prototype changes |

**FactSnapshot v0** (full text: `crates/ep9_facts/tests/golden/schema.txt`): [2026-11-04, M6-P3 B1W; FREEZE-M1 B1: the schema as M6-P0 SCHEMA6 built it] The block below is the schema 1 summary. `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` 2 (`crates/ep9_facts/src/lib.rs`; `schema-history.txt` line 2) keeps the header, the ref forms and the record key, and adds: a ShapeDesc `elements` byte (an ElementsKind + 1, 0 = none) and, in each key's attrs byte, the key's field hint (FieldRep bits 4-6, const bit 7); per record a RecordFlags byte (exited, ran) and, in a PROFILED or HINTED snapshot only, a confidence byte (H30-Q1); in KeyedFacts a KeyedHint byte (array, object, string, primitive, out-of-bounds, hole: the keyed bits 4-9) and a u16 ElementsKindSet (bit i = elements kind value i); ObjectKind arguments 7 and typed-array 8; the ElementsKind values in the VM order (H18 "FactSnapshot kind list"); and four open lists (ElementsKind, FieldRep, ShapeFlags, ElementsKindSet): a value or bit that a reader does not name reads as none (FieldRep: any) or is dropped, for that field only, never a refusal of the snapshot. The open-list names are outside the fingerprint, so an append is a fill and moves no version; any other format change moves `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` (the Rules row). The Access tags getter and setter are in the schema since version 1; nothing writes them yet ("Declared, no writer" in Fact meanings).

```text
header 64 B: magic "\x89EP9FS\r\n" | schema_version | bytecode_abi | unit_hash[32] | source | 0 0 0
             | shape_count | record_count | total_size              (u32 little endian)
shapes:  distinct ShapeDesc {ProtoRef, ObjectKind, flags, capacity u16, [(KeyRef, attrs)]}, ascending
records: (function u32, slot u32, kind u8, payload(kind)), ascending; key = (unit_hash, function, slot)
ProtoRef  = null | intrinsic index (H13) | prototype of FunctionRef | unknown
FunctionRef = js (unit hash, function id >= 1) | builtin (BuiltinId u16)
KeyRef    = string (Latin-1 or UTF-16) | well-known symbol | other symbol
```

- No type can hold a ShapeId, an address, a cell kind id or a closure identity. The VM exporter (`ep9_vm::abi::export_facts`, H16-Q5, M0 month 3) turns a live shape into a ShapeDesc.
- Bitsets (TypeSet, BinaryHint, CompareHint, ForInHint, KeySet, BindingSet) only grow and merge by OR. H30 sets precedence between sources.

## Rules

| Rule | Check | From |
|---|---|---|
| Slot kinds are ABI bytes 0-20; a change bumps `ABI_VERSION` and `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` | `ep9_bytecode/tests/abi.rs`; `ep9_facts/tests/schema.rs` | M0 wk4 |
| A new slot kind does not compile until it has a payload | exhaustive matches: `Payload::of`, `SlotFacts::empty`, the decoder | M0 wk4 |
| Any format change bumps `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` | `schema-history.txt`: SHA-256 of `schema.txt` + the sample bytes; refuses a changed fingerprint even under `EP9_BLESS` | M0 wk4 |
| `ep9_facts` depends on `ep9_bytecode` only; no unsafe | dep-guards check 7; `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` | M0 wk4 |
| One encoding per fact set; the same facts in any build order give the same bytes | `tests/encoding.rs` (two build orders, corrupt input, 3,000 random round trips) | M0 wk4 |
| A record whose kind is not the unit's `feedback_slots[i]` is dropped; another ABI or unit hash drops the snapshot | `tests/validate.rs` | M0 wk4 |
| IC and feedback data hold shapes weakly | `ep9_vm` tests: a dead shape clears its entry, a dead holder misses (`tests/feedback.rs`, `tests/ic.rs`) | M0 wk4 |
| Prototype-chain cases compare `proto_epoch`; a value store to a prototype does not bump it, so a prototype load reads the holder at every hit. **M4-P1 (K5):** they keep the validity cell of the receiver shape's prototype instead; a structural change of a prototype on that chain clears it, a value store or a change off the chain does not | `tests/ic.rs`: a replaced method, a read-only `x` on Object.prototype, a missing property that appears; from M4-P1 `tests/validity.rs`: a change below a cached holder, changes off the chain, realms, the GC of prototypes and of users, no saturation, the depth limit, a random walk for the invariant and soundness | M0 wk4; cells M4-P1 |
| IC hit paths do not allocate or call; T0 fast handlers inline them | `cargo xtask disasm-check` (tail handlers) | M0 wk4 |
| ICs never change test262 results | T262 and T262-EAGER give the same sets | M0 wk4 |
| No code patching: IC state lives in RW data | review of JIT and AOT IC code | M5 / A3 |

## Consequences

- **Gains.** One IC entry and one fact schema for T0, JIT, AOT and profiles. No ABI bump in week 4: the 88 BytecodeGen goldens and `abi-history.txt` stay.
- **Costs.** A ShapeDesc is larger than a ShapeId, and it is a prediction: two live shapes can share one description (for example with an `unknown` prototype), so a consumer always guards. Per-function feedback merges closures (H16-Q1).
- **Follow-ups.** `.ep9prof` (H16-Q5b, A3); the H13 index lists that `intrinsic` and `builtin` refs name (H16-Q4); H30 merge rules.

| [R17] | ShapeIds are stable only for snapshot root shapes; other guards use RW cells | [17-aot-refutation.md](../research/17-aot-refutation.md) |

## Validation

- `cargo test -p ep9_facts`: round trip, golden bytes, history, wrong-kind drop, two build orders. Negative controls on 2026-10-21: a 22nd kind fails to compile in 3 matches; a renamed kind and an encoder change fail the history test under `EP9_BLESS=1`.
- SP-Facts in Spike C (M1): one kernel compiled from a PROFILED FactSnapshot gives identical output at ≥ 90% of live-feedback speed.

## Open questions

| ID | Question | Needed by | Proposed default |
|---|---|---|---|
| H16-Q1 | Per-closure FeedbackCell (V8) or per-function feedback | M1 | Per-function; revisit if realms or contexts make sites polymorphic. **Filled 2026-10-27 (M4-P2 ADR-A0; plan v9 V9-D11): per function, as proposed and as built.** One FEEDBACK cell per FunctionInfo, reached from `FunctionInfo.feedback` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/function_info.rs:74-77`), shared by every closure of the function and by every realm (a FunctionInfo is per isolate, H22). Facts, `.ep9prof` and unit IC cells key a function by (unit hash, function id) (K8; FunctionRef), so one vector per function matches them; a call key never leaves the process ([13 §4.3](../plan/13-execution-modes.md#43-rules) F-R8). The cost (Consequences: closures merge) stays watched by the third "Reopens if" row. No code, test or golden changes |
| H16-Q2 | When T0 records BinaryOp, CompareOp, Call and the other non-property kinds | M1 | M1, with 09 §2.2 "T0 records every H16 slot kind" **Filled 2026-10-26 (M4-P1 PROFILE): from IC_LAYOUT 2, with three cost rules (V9-D6).** `crates/ep9_vm/src/profile.rs` writes the value half (an int32) of BinaryOp, CompareOp, TypeOf, the keyed kinds (2, 4, 6, 10, 17), Call at `Call*` sites, JumpLoop, ForIn and CreateClosure; its module docs hold the table (bits, key half, when). A slot is written only when a bit or state is new. (1) **Implicit int32:** an int32 pair with an int32 result (BinaryOp), an int32 operand (unary), an int32 pair (CompareOp) and a present int32 index of an Array (`GetKeyedProperty`) write nothing; the reader adds those bits to every slot of its kind in a function with a vector. An int32 `+`/`-` that overflows stays implicit; `*`, `/`, `>>>`, `%`, `**` record `INT32_OVERFLOW`. A unary op records no overflow. (2) **Sampled (plan R2 "cold path only"; a V9-D6 waiver):** Call records only in the first 64 budget units after each poll (`CALL_WINDOW`), JumpLoop only at a back edge that polls; counts saturate at `i32::MAX`. The Call key is the native word of the callee's FunctionInfo (FUNCTION cell +24; no barrier, never dies, two closures are one target), or the hole for a second target or a non-FUNCTION callee: not the function cell of the Decision table. (3) **Keyed kinds record on their cold path only**; the key half is left to KEYED (M4-P3). ForIn keeps the receiver shape weakly (with the barrier) and `ForInHint::ANY`. CreateClosure counts to 2 (many). Cost (mac a64, instructions, median of 5, `EP9_HASH_SEED=1`, vs d26c819): t0-loop +0.26%, t0-branchy +0.09%, t0-call +0.05%, t0-prop +0.94%, ci-v0 +0.34%; over 1% (waiver): richards +6.66%, deltablue +1.23%, crypto +7.46%, earley-boyer +2.16%, splay +2.04%, navier-stokes +17.76% (number BinaryOp and CompareOp records, and cold keyed records: keyed is cold until KEYED's fast path). x86-64 is not measured: there 22 recording handlers (`Add`, `Sub`, the compares, `TypeOf`, ...) save one or two registers on entry (`disasm-check -v`); `JumpLoop` and `AddSmi` do not. [Revised 2026-10-26, M4-P1 review R-H16-P1-1 and R-H16-P1-2: against the same tree with every hook empty, the 22 x86-64 handlers save 1 to 4 registers at entry, so the int32 cases pay it too: 4 in `Div`, `DivSmi`, `TestEqualStrict` and the four relational compares; 3 in `ForInNext`; 2 in `Add`, `Sub`, `Mul`, `MulSmi`, `Negate`, `ToNumber`, `ToNumeric`, `TestEqual`, `ForInPrepare`, `CallWithSpread`; 1 in `Inc`, `Dec`, `BitwiseNot`, `TypeOf` (aarch64: no frame change); the x86-64 cost is the orchestrator's decision P1-ORCH-3. The cost list above is against d26c819, where raytrace did not run; against the same tree (33b0eac) with every hook empty, raytrace is +1.38% (mac a64, the same counter), and the waiver is split (P1-ORCH-2b: the keyed part W-PROFILE-1a ends at the KEYED merge; the BinaryOp NUMBER and CompareOp part W-PROFILE-1b is accepted, with a month-5 row).] [Revised 2026-10-26, M4-P1 PROFILE-X64 (P1-ORCH-3): the fast handlers no longer hold record code. A record leaves the handler as a `Flow` exit (`Record`, `RecordPair`, `JumpRecordCall`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/mod.rs`): in the sibling expansion a tail call of an out-of-line function (`record_next`, `record_pair_next`, `record_call_next`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/dsl.rs`) that takes one constant word (the bits, and the slot operand's place) and finds the next pc itself; a Call records after the frame push. TypeOf, ForInNext, CallWithSpread, BitwiseNot and the non-number unary and compare cases record on their cold path; the binary and relational fast bodies are the hooks-off bodies, with the bits chosen before the operation. The facts recorded are the same (`tests::profile`, `tests::feedback`). `disasm-check -v` (5 targets, 284 handlers, 282 tail): the 22 handlers save no register at entry on x86-64 (Linux and Windows), and every target's list of handlers with a frame is the hooks-off list; cold handlers save more (the records moved there). Int32 path, instructions (shortest path from entry to the dispatch jump; hooks off / before / after): x86-64 Linux `Add` 21/26/21, `TestLessThan` 22/31/21, `Inc` 11/13/12; aarch64 `Add` 19/17/17, `TestLessThan` 23/22/19, `Inc` 12/12/10. Cost (mac a64, the same counter, against 16582a4 with every hook empty): t0-loop -2.80%, t0-branchy -3.55%, t0-call -0.03%, t0-prop -0.23%, ci-v0 -0.59%, richards +7.78%, crypto +4.91%, navier-stokes +17.96% (16582a4: +7.07%, +7.46%, +17.62%). x86-64 is still not measured at run time.] [Added 2026-10-27, M4-P1 records review R-RF-1 (the P1-ORCH-3 result): x86-64 cachegrind (sandbox, perf-ci-v0, instructions) at fc77622 against d26c819 / against the hooks-off build of 4c2d101: t0-loop +0.28 / +0.27%, t0-branchy -1.87 / -1.74%, t0-call +0.93 / +0.93%, t0-prop +0.40 / +0.80%, ci-v0 -0.07 / +0.76%, richards +9.29 / +9.44%, deltablue -0.93 / +1.31%. The gate set is within +1.0% on x86-64; richards is the CompareOp record cost of W-PROFILE-1b.] Tests: `tests::profile` (16) in `crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/profile.rs`. [2026-10-29, M5-P1 integration fixer, from PROFILE5: (1) since MODES-D16 (M5-P1) an int32 `+`, `-`, `Inc` or `Dec` whose result is outside int32 records `INT32_OVERFLOW` on its overflow arm, so "An int32 `+`/`-` that overflows stays implicit" and "A unary op records no overflow" above describe M4 only (Fact meanings item 2). (2) Since the M5-P1 window jitter a poll resets the budget to `BUDGET` plus 0..127 units, so the Call window is the 64 units after that jitter, not the first 64 after the poll (Fact meanings, sampled counts).] [2026-11-04, M6-P3 B1W; FREEZE-M1 B1; doc 13 C-27, MODES-D11, MODES-D19, M-R9; a clarify of the 2026-10-29 PROFILE6 row:] This fill holds under the type-feedback mode `full` (the JIT and hybrid presets; a training run, doc 13 §1.2): T0 runs the `profile.rs` record hooks there. Under `ics_only` (the jitless default) it runs none of them, and the export leaves out the kinds that come only from them (the 2026-11-01 rewrite row). `ics_only` drops only those record hooks: the T0 ICs and their IC-side counts (Literal, CloneObject, Construct), the allocation-site elements kind (the ALLOC_SITE handler) and every other object-model input stay, so arrays get the same kinds and objects the same shapes in jitless as in JIT (doc 13 M-R9; `ep9_builtins` `tests/presets_object_model.rs`). The source line "T0 records every H16 slot kind" (09 §2.2) holds for `full`; its `ics_only` condition is a plan fix for the plan owner. The tuning values of this cell (the implicit cases, `CALL_WINDOW`, the jitter, the sampled kinds) are in the Freeze-table part "recording policy (gated)" (A4 gate); its meanings are in "fact meanings". |
| H16-Q3 | Megamorphic stub cache layout | M1 | A per-isolate (ShapeId, key) table flushed at every GC (H8) **Filled 2026-10-27 (M4-P3 IC2; plan v9 §5.3, OI-09): as proposed, with the operation in the key.** `crates/ep9_vm/src/stub_cache.rs`: one table per isolate (`Runtime::stub_cache`), 1024 entries of (key = operation << 32 \| receiver ShapeId, name = the atom's bits, handler), direct-mapped by a hash of the three, in Rust memory. The operation is load (0x21, 0x22), store (0x24) or define (0x25): a field a load reads is not always one a store may write or a define keeps, and a 0x24 add checks the chain while a 0x25 add does not. The handlers are the K9-K13 handlers (smi field and `ARRAY_LENGTH`, `PROTO_LOAD` and `MISSING` with their validity cell, `ADD`), so a hit runs the same handler code as a case of the site's own. Probe: on the miss path (`ic::mega_load`, `ic::mega_store`, called by `slow::get_named`, `get_super`, `set_named` before the generic op), not in the hit path, where it would cost every monomorphic hit two more loads (+1.4% instructions on t0-prop). `SLOW` cases and strings (ShapeId 0) are never stored. Fill: the miss path of a megamorphic entry computes the case for an object receiver (`ic::skip_miss`, `ic::MEGA_FILL`), and `ic::record` puts it in the table, after its last allocation. Weak: the GC does not trace the table; a weak source of the heap empties it in every collection, before the sweep (one flag test when nothing was put in since the last GC), so a ShapeId or atom address that a dead cell frees never matches an old entry. Not exported: compiled code reaches a megamorphic site through `Generic` (CacheIR op lists). Probe (`ic-stats`, mac a64, EP9_HASH_SEED=1): raytrace megamorphic load misses that reach the generic path 1,598,234 -> 368 (all at one site, the anonymous constructor's `this.initialize` of `Class.create`; stub cache hits 1,597,866); instructions -2.2% (raytrace). Tests: `tests::stub_cache` (9). The InstanceOf slot (14) is filled by `crates/ep9_vm/src/instance_ic.rs` in the same phase (IC_LAYOUT 2 INSTANCEOF handler; `tests::instance_ic`, 9). **Note 2026-10-27 (M4-P4 integration fixer A; MODES-CHECK-w15 MC-w15-7a):** the size (1024), the hash and the probe place (the miss path; compiled code through `Generic`) are tuning values in the doc 13 C-25 form, which freeze at the A4 gate, not with this row at M1 exit; the operation set (load, store, define) is append-only; the flush rule is "at least at every major GC" (the table is emptied at every collection today, minor ones included, which meets it). **Note 2026-10-27 (M4-P4 integration fixer C; Codex D2-1):** replaces the flush rule just above. The table is emptied at every collection, minor ones included. A table kept across a minor GC needs first a minor-GC weak filter of every cell an entry names: `ic::record` can make a young IC_HANDLER that only the table names, a minor GC would free it while its receiver shape and name still match, and `ic::load_with` would read freed memory. **Note 2026-10-27 (M4-P4 integration fixer C; Codex B1-1, CX-C-D1):** the instanceof hit walk takes 8 interrupt budget units after each full group of 8 chain steps and misses when the budget is used up; the cold path's generic walk then polls (H23 "Interrupt poll"). A walk that returns inside a group takes no unit for that group, so the last 1 to 8 steps of a walk take none, and a walk that uses up the budget runs up to 8 steps past it. The FREEZE-M1 batch-1 writer moves these into an H16 Freeze table. **Note 2026-10-28 (M4-P5 modes fixer; MODES-CHECK-w16 MC-w16-5):** the group size 8 (`WALK_STEPS`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/instance_ic.rs`) is a speed value (+1.2% instructions with a check per group, +7.2% with a check per step, on a monomorphic 20-deep walk). It is a tuning value in the doc 13 C-25 form, which freezes at the A4 gate, not with this cell at M1 exit. The rule is: at most one group of steps per walk goes uncharged (8 today), and a walk that uses up the budget misses after at most one group past it, so the generic walk polls |
| H16-Q4 | The index lists behind `intrinsic` and `builtin` refs | M1 (H13 lists) | H13 stable indices; BuiltinId = block (7 bits) + index (9 bits) **Filled 2026-11-04 (M6-P3 B1W, FREEZE-M1 B1): as proposed and as built.** A ProtoRef `intrinsic` holds an `IntrinsicId` value (the H13 list "Realm intrinsics"; golden `crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/vm-builtin-ids.txt`); the exporter names a prototype by the intrinsic of the current realm that it is (`facts_export.rs` `proto_ref`), so an id that names no object (72, the realm FUSE cell) never matches. A FunctionRef `builtin` holds a `BuiltinId` u16: block (7 bits) and index in the block (9 bits) (`crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs`; the H13 list "Builtins", the goldens of `builtin_ids` and `table`). Both lists are append-only (H13 Rules row 1). A FactSnapshot names no engine build (its header holds the bytecode ABI and the unit hash), so these ids must keep their meaning across builds: that is the H13-Q1 rule for the ids that facts, hints and profiles store (FREEZE-M1 B3) |
| H16-Q5 | VM exporter (FeedbackVector → FactSnapshot). Split 2026-10-25 (V8-A21, M3-PLAN-D54): the `.ep9prof` container half is H16-Q5b | Month 2 (built M0 month 3) | Walk a live shape to its root to build the ShapeDesc. **Filled 2026-10-25 (M3-P3 FACTS-EXPORT): as proposed.** `ep9_vm::abi::export_facts(&Isolate, &UnitHash) -> Result<FactSnapshot, &'static str>` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs`; public by V8-A13, not in the H20 core) reads the first loaded unit with that content hash (else an error) and writes one record per feedback slot that a T0 IC site uses, in each function that has a vector: PropertyFacts for 0x21, 0x22, 0x24, 0x25 and GlobalFacts for 0x10-0x12. Other kinds get no record (the exporter reads only these kinds; T0 records the others since M4-P1 except InstanceOf, which it records from IC2 (M4-P3), H16-Q2, and FACTS2 exports them in M4-P2), and a function with no vector gets none. Header: this build's bytecode ABI, the unit hash, source `runtime`; `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1. IC state = the K3 entry state. Handlers: own field → `own-field`, `ARRAY_LENGTH` → `array-length`, `SLOW` → `generic`, `PROTO_LOAD` → `proto-field` (depth = chain steps to the holder), `MISSING` → `missing` (depth = prototypes to null), `ADD` → `add-field`; a global case gives `lexical`, `data-property` or (a `SLOW` global-object case) `accessor-property`. [Revised 2026-10-25, M3-P3 fix round 2, Codex C-FX1: the binding comes from what made the case, not from the key's prototype, because the global object can have a null prototype too. A field case is `lexical` and a GLOBAL cell `data-property`. A `SLOW` case is `accessor-property` for an accessor (a lexical binding is never one) and `data-property` for a data property under a shape whose prototype is not null; under a null prototype it is `lexical` or `data-property` only if its key is the current shape of the lexical record or of the global object (not both), else it gives no binding.] [Added 2026-10-25, M3-P4 ACCEPT-8 review: a `SLOW` case whose key is a dictionary-mode shape gives no binding, also for an accessor: the name can be anywhere in a dictionary table, so `Exporter::global` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs`) reads no `SLOW` case of a dictionary shape. A probe (not committed): a function that reads a global accessor gives `accessor-property`; when the global object is a dictionary (a `delete` of a property that is not its last one, `props::to_dictionary`), the same read gives no binding.] A live shape becomes a ShapeDesc by its root walk: its keys in property order with their attributes (the descriptor prefix of the shape, equal to the parent chain); its prototype is `null`, an intrinsic of the current realm by its `IntrinsicId` value (until the H16-Q4 lists), the initial `prototype` of a bytecode function (by unit hash and function id), or `unknown`. [Revised 2026-10-25, M3-P3 fix round 2, Codex C-FX2: `function-prototype` needs a proof that the VM keeps, not the `constructor` and `prototype` backlinks, which JS can write (`C.prototype = {constructor: C}`, an arrow function given a `prototype`). Only a class constructor has one: DefineClass makes its own `prototype` {w: false, c: false} and sets its `[[HomeObject]]` to that object, and no JS can change either. A `function` declaration's lazy `prototype` is writable and the VM keeps no proof of the initial object, so it is `unknown`, until a VM change keeps one.] No ShapeId, address or closure identity is written. [Added 2026-10-25, Codex C-FX3: the exporter keeps no table keyed by a heap-cell Value (H12-Q2 (a)); it describes each shape and prototype again where a case names it.] [Added 2026-10-25, M3-P3 fix round 3, Codex C2-FX1: a finished ShapeDesc is interned by its content (a set of `Arc<ShapeDesc>`, no heap address in the key), so equal descriptions share one `Arc` and the memory kept grows with the distinct descriptions, not with the sites. Test `equal_descriptions_share_one_arc` in `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/facts_export.rs`: 256 sites that read one shape with a 64 KiB key share one description.] A case with no description is left out (a state may have fewer cases than the IC had): the string key, a dead key, an object kind with no ObjectKind, a stale prototype case (epoch changed) or a dead holder or target, and receivers with one description but two accesses. Value TypeSets stay empty (H16-Q2). Not a GC point; runs no JS. Tests: `tests::facts_export` (5) in `crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/facts_export.rs` (each handler, states, left-out cases, the root walk, equal bytes from two isolates) and `richards_facts_round_trip` in `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/facts_export.rs` (richards: export, encode, decode, equal; the validator drops nothing; a second isolate gives equal bytes). [Revised 2026-10-25, fix round 2: `tests::facts_export` has 7 tests (added: an old null-prototype `SLOW` key gives no binding; no cell-keyed table in the exporter), and `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/facts_export.rs` has 3 (added, each with and without GC stress: a null-prototype global's accessor and read-only cases are not `lexical`; only a class gives `function-prototype`, and the replaced, forged, arrow and `function`-declaration cases give `unknown`). Richards makes its objects with `function` declarations, so its receivers now have `unknown` prototypes.] The CLI flag `--facts-out` is M3-P4 FIXES |
| H16-Q5b | The `.ep9prof` container: the file that a PGO training run writes (`--profile-out`) and AOT reads | A3 (docs/plan/02-architecture.md:227: PGO capture is A3) | A container of `ep9_facts` snapshot bytes (`FactSnapshot::encode`), one per unit hash, with source `profiled`; decided with the A3 PGO capture |

## CacheIR op lists (V9-D9)

Filled 2026-10-27 (M4-P2 ADR-A0; plan v9 V9-D9, OI-10). The plan row asks for "CacheIR-like guard/action lists" (Decision). The form:

- **T0 keeps its handlers.** The int32 handlers and the IC_HANDLER and POLY_IC cells of K9-K18 stay the T0 form, so a T0 hit stays one compare and one load (K9). [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P2 integration fixer B, review R-CI-5: was "T0 never runs an op list".] T0 runs no op list today; a later list reader in T0 or GenInterp is a fill.
- **The op list is the shared form for JIT, AOT and facts.** A reader that needs more than T0 (T1, T2, unit code, the facts exporter, the stub cache) maps one IC case to one list with one total function, `ops_of(case, operation)`: guards first, then one action. The operation is the site's (load, store or define, from the site opcode), because one handler kind can serve more than one: a `GLOBAL` case serves the loads 0x10, 0x11 and the store 0x12 (`ic::global_case(rt, name, store)`; `ic::store_global` writes through it). Each pair of handler kind and operation maps to a fixed list, so the list adds no RW state. [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P2 Codex-fix docs agent, P2-ORCH-9 (Codex round 1, ADR-A0 slice): was `ops_of(case)`, one list per handler kind.]
- **Refs, not pointers.** A list names heap things through the H30 ref nodes (an append-only list in H30; today the five RootRef, IntrinsicRef, ShapeRef, CellRef, UnitConst) [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P4 integration fixer A, MC-w15-10a: was "the five H30 ref nodes"], never through a raw address, so the same list serves JIT finalize and unit finalize (H34). [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P2 integration fixer B, review R-CI-3: was "(ShapeRef, CellRef, RootRef)".] The FunctionRef, ProtoRef, holder ref and record ref of the ops below are names (FunctionRef and ProtoRef are the H16 FactSnapshot forms), not ref nodes of their own: the ref node or H34 relocation that each one lowers to is fixed with the first reader that emits it (a fill).
- **Append-only.** Ops and handler kinds form append-only lists ([13 §5.4](../plan/13-execution-modes.md#54-room-in-the-contracts-append-only-parts-and-gated-parts), C-24): a new op or a new handler kind is a fill when its first reader is built; an existing op never changes its meaning; a reader that meets an unknown op treats the case as a miss (generic path).

| Op (v0 list) | Kind | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| GuardShape(obj, ShapeRef) | guard | word0 ShapeId of obj = the shape (the K9 compare) |
| GuardProtoValidity(ProtoRef) | guard | the prototype chain of the case is still valid: in T0 the handler's VALIDITY_CELL (K5); in compiled and unit code a Dynamic FuseRef of the descriptor kind "prototype-chain validity by ProtoRef" (H31-Q1), never the cell itself, because an invalid cell stays invalid and the relearned case gets a new one (H17 validity row). [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P2 integration fixer B, review R-CI-1: was GuardValidity(CellRef).] |
| GuardKind(obj, kind) | guard | the cell kind byte (array, string; later an elements kind, H18) |
| GuardType(value, int32 / number / string / symbol / bigint / heap) | guard | the value's type (BinaryOp, CompareOp, keyed key types). heap: a value that is not a number (a cell or another immediate), the check of the FIELD_HEAP_* stores (`ic_ops.rs` `Ty::Heap`). [Revised 2026-11-03, M6-P1 integration fixer (CBC need 5): heap appended by M6-P1 CBC] |
| GuardTarget(callee, FunctionRef) | guard | the call target (Call, InstanceOf) |
| GuardIndex(obj, key) | guard | an in-bounds index, not a hole (keyed) |
| GuardSlotsRoom(obj, location) | guard | the object's SLOTS cell exists and has room at the location (an out-of-line ADD: T0 stores only into room the SLOTS cell has, else it misses, `crates/ep9_vm/src/ic.rs` `store`, the ADD case). [Added 2026-10-27, M4-P2 integration fixer B, review R-CI-2] |
| GuardNotHole(value) | guard | a lexical binding is not the TDZ hole (the lexical-record case of a global site: T0 misses on the hole). [Added 2026-10-27, M4-P2 integration fixer B, review R-CI-4] |
| LoadField(obj, location) / StoreField(obj, location) | action | an in-object or SLOTS field (FIELD_IN_OBJECT, FIELD_OUT_OF_LINE) |
| LoadHolderField(holder ref, location) | action | a field of a prototype holder (PROTO_LOAD) |
| LoadHolderDict(holder ref, key) | action | the data entry of a dictionary-mode holder for the site's name, looked up at each hit; a missing or accessor entry is a miss (PROTO_DICT_LOAD; H17 Dictionary mode row). [Added 2026-11-03, M6-P1 ACCIC] |
| AddField(obj, ShapeRef, location) | action | the K13 add: store, then the new shape (H21 P3 order) |
| LoadArrayLength / LoadStringLength | action | ARRAY_LENGTH, STRING_LENGTH |
| LoadUndefined | action | MISSING |
| LoadGlobalField(record ref, location) | action | GLOBAL (with GuardShape on the global object and the lexical record) |
| StoreGlobalField(record ref, location) | action | GLOBAL at a store (0x12 `StaGlobal`): one store of the value into the global object's field, through the write barrier (H11), as `ic::store_global` does (`object::wr`). A store case exists only for a writable data property: `ic::global_case` makes a read-only one `SLOW`. [Added 2026-10-27, M4-P2 Codex-fix docs agent, P2-ORCH-9] |
| LoadElement(kind) / StoreElement(kind) | action | keyed, per elements kind (KEYED, M4-P3; H18) |
| CallTarget(FunctionRef) / CallGetter / CallSetter | action | monomorphic calls; accessor ICs (H17 Accessor ICs row) |
| Generic | action | SLOW and MEGA: the generic operation (megamorphic: the stub cache, H16-Q3) |
| BinaryArith(op, int32 / number) | action | `lhs op rhs` on operands of the guarded type, as T0's fast body computes it: an int32 pair gives the int32 result that fits, else the exact double (an overflow, `*`, `/`, `>>>` past `i32::MAX`); a number pair (`+`, `-`, `*`, `/` only) gives the double. It misses on an int32 `%` with a negative dividend or a 0 divisor (BinaryOp). [Added 2026-11-03, M6-P1 CBC] |
| UnaryArith(op, int32 / number) | action | `++`, `--`, unary `-`, `~`, ToNumber or ToNumeric (the unary opcodes on a BinaryOp slot) on an operand of the guarded type: an int32 gives the int32 result that fits, else the double; a number (not `~`) gives the double. [Added 2026-11-03, M6-P1 CBC] |
| CompareNumbers(op, int32 / number) | action | `==`, `===`, `<`, `>`, `<=` or `>=` of two operands of the guarded type (IEEE rules for doubles: NaN compares false, -0 equals 0) (CompareOp). [Added 2026-11-03, M6-P1 CBC] |
| OrdinaryHasInstance(field) | action | OrdinaryHasInstance (§7.3.21 steps 3-6) with P = the rhs function's field at this index: a primitive gives false; a miss when P is not an object, on a Proxy in the chain, or when the chain walk uses up the interrupt budget (`instance_ic::hit`) (INSTANCEOF). [Added 2026-11-03, M6-P1 CBC; text by the M6-P1 integration fixer] |

| Handler (T0 form) | `ops_of` |
|---|---|
| FIELD_IN_OBJECT, FIELD_OUT_OF_LINE | GuardShape, LoadField or StoreField |
| PROTO_LOAD | GuardShape, GuardProtoValidity, LoadHolderField |
| MISSING | GuardShape, GuardProtoValidity, LoadUndefined |
| PROTO_LOAD, MISSING under `KEY_STRING` (a string's method load; M4-P3 IC2, K11) | GuardKind (string), GuardProtoValidity (the chain from %String.prototype%), then LoadHolderField or LoadUndefined. GuardKind, not GuardShape: a string has no shape, and the kind check keeps a symbol (ShapeId 0 too) off the case. [Added 2026-10-27, M4-P3 integration fixer, IC2 need 3] |
| ADD | GuardShape, GuardProtoValidity (0x24 only), GuardSlotsRoom (out of line only), AddField |
| GLOBAL | GuardShape (global object), GuardShape (lexical record), then LoadGlobalField (load: 0x10, 0x11) or StoreGlobalField (store: 0x12). [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P2 Codex-fix docs agent, P2-ORCH-9: was LoadGlobalField for every operation, so a store would have been compiled as a load] |
| smi field keyed on the lexical record (0x10-0x12) | GuardShape (lexical record), then LoadField and GuardNotHole on the value (load), or GuardNotHole on the old value and StoreField (store) |
| ARRAY_LENGTH / STRING_LENGTH | GuardKind, LoadArrayLength / LoadStringLength. T0 keys ARRAY_LENGTH on the array's shape; GuardKind is a deliberate widening (any ARRAY has its int32 `length` at +24), sound for every array shape |
| Call slot of `Call*` (0x5e-0x66), one target key | GuardTarget(callee, FunctionRef), CallTarget(FunctionRef). The key is the target's FunctionInfo (the F-R8 call key), so the guard holds for every closure of one function. An empty or megamorphic slot (undefined, the hole), `CallWithSpread` and `Construct*` give Generic (a list for them is a fill). CallTarget misses where T0's `call_fast` goes cold for a reason the guard cannot see: another realm, a sloppy target with a primitive receiver, a class constructor, interrupt due, the call that makes the target's vector, a full VM stack. [Added 2026-11-03, M6-P1 CBC] |
| BinaryOp slot (0x2a-0x41; the unary 0x42-0x45, 0x59, 0x5a) | From the BinaryHint bits (int32 is implicit: 0 is "int32 or never ran", F-R9). Only INT32 or INT32_OVERFLOW: GuardType(lhs, int32), GuardType(rhs, int32) (not for the Smi forms 0x36-0x41, whose rhs is an immediate), BinaryArith(op, int32). With NUMBER too: the same with number, for `+`, `-`, `*`, `/` only. `**`, `%` and the bitwise and shift operators on numbers, and any other hint bit, give Generic (T0 has no fast arm for them). The unary forms: GuardType(operand, int32 or number), UnaryArith (`~` has no number list). [Added 2026-11-03, M6-P1 CBC] |
| CompareOp slot (0x4b-0x50) | From the CompareHint bits (int32 is implicit). Only INT32: GuardType(lhs, int32), GuardType(rhs, int32), CompareNumbers(op, int32). With NUMBER too: the same with number, for `===` and the four relational operators. `==` on numbers, `== null`, the identity arm of `===` and T0's arm for two atoms (M6-P1 CBC) have no list (a list for them is a fill); any other hint bit gives Generic. [Added 2026-11-03, M6-P1 CBC] |
| SLOW, MEGA | Generic |
| CONSTRUCT_SITE, CLONE (IC_LAYOUT 2) | no list in v0: allocation-site cases are read as facts (CallFacts, CloneFacts) until a tier needs a list; a list for them is a fill. [Revised 2026-11-03, M6-P1 integration fixer (CBC need 4): INSTANCEOF has its own row below; was "CONSTRUCT_SITE, CLONE, INSTANCEOF (IC_LAYOUT 2)" and "allocation-site and instanceof cases are read as facts (CallFacts, CloneFacts, TargetFacts)"] |
| FIELD_INT32_*, FIELD_NUMBER_*, FIELD_HEAP_* (9-14; M5-P2 CONSTREP) | as FIELD_IN_OBJECT and FIELD_OUT_OF_LINE at a store, with a check of the value first: GuardType(value, int32) or GuardType(value, number); the HEAP check is GuardType(value, heap) (M6-P1 CBC). [Revised 2026-11-03, M6-P1 integration fixer (CBC need 5): was "the HEAP check (no number) has no GuardType value yet, so its list is a fill with its first compiled reader"] |
| GETTER, SETTER (IC_LAYOUT 4; M6-P1 ACCIC; H17 Accessor ICs) | own accessor (+16 int32 0): GuardShape, then CallGetter (load) or CallSetter (0x24 store) on the pair in the receiver's field at the location; inherited (+16 the validity cell): GuardShape, GuardProtoValidity, then CallGetter or CallSetter on the pair in the holder's field. At 0x22 the receiver of these ops is the lookup start; CallGetter passes the site's `this` value as `this` (T0: `get_super`). The pair is read at each hit, never baked; a guard on a pair identity is a fill with its first compiled reader. T0 runs these lists in the cold body, not in a hit path (no call in a hit path) [Added 2026-11-03, M6-P1 ACCIC] |
| PROTO_DICT_LOAD (IC_LAYOUT 4; M6-P1 ACCIC; H17 Dictionary mode row) | GuardShape, GuardProtoValidity, LoadHolderDict. T0 runs it in the cold body. A receiver in dictionary mode stays SLOW (K12) [Added 2026-11-03, M6-P1 ACCIC] |
| INSTANCEOF (IC_LAYOUT 2; M6-P1 CBC) | GuardTarget(rhs function, FunctionRef), GuardShape(rhs function, ShapeRef: the shape at the handler's +24), GuardProtoValidity(the rhs function's prototype chain: the validity cell at +16 in T0), OrdinaryHasInstance(field: the `prototype` field index at +32). The facts form stays TargetFacts. [Added 2026-11-03, M6-P1 CBC; text by the M6-P1 integration fixer (CBC need 4)] |

The smi handlers are in `crates/ep9_vm/src/ic.rs:139-230`, the IC_HANDLER kinds in `crates/ep9_vm/src/ic.rs:233-279`. `ops_of` is built (M6-P1 CBC, `crates/ep9_vm/src/ic_ops.rs`); only the tests (`tests::ic_ops`, `tests::accessor_ic`) call it until a compiled tier reads it. [Revised 2026-11-03, M6-P1 integration fixer (CBC need 3): was "Nothing is built: the first `ops_of` comes with its first reader (month 5-6 ICs, OI-15, or Spike C)."] The op list is not a FactSnapshot format, so it needs no `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. [2026-11-04, M6-P3 B1W; FREEZE-M1 B1; MODES-CHECK MC-w25-3:] Two rules for the first compiled reader of `ops_of` (T1). (1) At 0x22 (K17) `Val::Receiver` is the lookup start, and `Operation::of` gives LoadNamed for 0x21 and 0x22 alike (`ic_ops.rs`), so the reader takes the site opcode too. The `this` of CallGetter and the receiver of the generic fallback of every 0x22 list (after a miss of a guard, of LoadHolderField or of LoadHolderDict: the super load, §13.3.7.3) are the site's `this`, never the lookup start, as T0 does (`slow::get_super`). `Val` has no name for that value today, so the reader either refuses 0x22 cases or appends a `Val` for the site's `this` that the action and its miss path both use (the V8 bug class of ead4e132f5). (2) An op that reads the holder at +24 (LoadHolderField, LoadHolderDict, and CallGetter or CallSetter of an inherited case) misses when the holder died, because the holder is weak (K7), as the T0 hits do (`tests::accessor_ic::a_cached_holder_is_weak`; the Rules row "a dead holder misses").

## Fact meanings: count units, call keys, implicit int32 (F-R7, F-R8, F-R9)

Filled 2026-10-27 (M4-P2 Codex-fix docs agent; orchestrator decision P2-ORCH-12; [13 §4.3](../plan/13-execution-modes.md#43-rules) F-R7, F-R8, F-R9 and C-06). This is what M4-P2 FACTS2 built; the exporter module doc says the same (`crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs:72-119`). No slot kind, schema or encoding changes (`FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1).

**Each count names its unit (F-R7; `crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs:96-110`).** A merge adds counts of one unit only (H16-Q5b). Every count saturates at `i32::MAX`.

| Field | Unit | Writer |
|---|---|---|
| `CallFacts.count`, `Call*` site | sampled calls: a call in the first `CALL_WINDOW` (64) budget units after a budget reset | `profile.rs` |
| `CallFacts.count`, `Construct*` site | constructs of the cold path (a hit writes nothing) | `construct_ic.rs` |
| `CallFacts.count`, a slot that a `Call*` and a `Construct*` site share | none: the count is 0 | - |
| `LoopFacts.back_edges` | sampled back edges: a back edge that polls | `profile.rs` |
| `LiteralFacts.count` | cold-path literal results: a lower bound of the allocations | `literal_site.rs` |

A sampled count is a share of the budget units, not of the executions. The windows come at a fixed period, so a call pattern with that period can stay out of every window (test `a_periodic_call_pattern_keeps_sites_out_of_the_fixed_window` in `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/facts_export.rs`); a jittered window start is a month-5 item. [Built 2026-10-28, M5-P1 PROFILE5: a poll resets the budget to `BUDGET` plus a jitter in 0..128 units from one fixed sequence (`profile::budget_after_poll`; every isolate and process runs the same sequence, so a second isolate gives the same facts), and a call is in the window when its budget before it is in (`BUDGET` - 64, `BUDGET`]. The test is now `a_periodic_call_pattern_reaches_every_site_through_the_jittered_window`: every one of the 99 sites gets a count.]

**A call key is never written out (F-R8; `crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs:111-119`).** T0 keys a `Call*` site by the native word of the callee's FunctionInfo and a `Construct*` site by the constructor cell (weak). The exporter branches on the site opcode and looks the FunctionInfo up by its address; it never reads the word as a pointer. A function of a loaded unit (not function 0) becomes `FunctionRef::Js {unit hash, id}`, a table builtin of the current realm `FunctionRef::Builtin(id)`. Anything else (a host function, a bound function, a proxy) makes the site megamorphic with no target. A slot that a `Call*` and a `Construct*` site share is megamorphic with count 0 (`crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs:440-453`). Tests `a_call_key_becomes_a_function_ref_or_megamorphic` and `a_shared_call_and_construct_slot_is_megamorphic` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/facts_export.rs`).

**The keyed kinds set: every miss, hits only when sampled (IC_LAYOUT 4 bits 10-25, bits 10-16 in IC_LAYOUT 3; M5-P1 ELEM1; `crates/ep9_vm/src/profile.rs` `record_keyed_cold`, `keyed_hit_kind`).** The value half of a keyed slot holds in bits 10-25 one bit per elements kind value (0-15, H18; IC_LAYOUT 3 held bits 10-16 for 0-6) of the receivers it saw before the access, for an index key on an Array or on an object of an `elements::plain_elements_kind` kind. Kinds 7-15 are reserved and append-only (`elements::kind`, OBJECT_LAYOUT 5; doc 13 F-R5): no kind uses them today. Since IC_LAYOUT 4 (M6-P0, M6-PLAN-D4) each has its bit already (bit 10 + value, `elements::KINDS_SET_MASK` = bits 10-25 for all 16 values), so a kind append moves no IC_LAYOUT. Bit 26 is the LoadKeyed "ran" bit (`profile::keyed::RAN`; MODES-D17, D18; declared, no writer yet) and bits 27-30 are reserved: no kind bit goes there. Every miss records the kind (the cold body). A fast hit (the `GetKeyedProperty` array path, the StoreKeyed store in place) records it only in the sampled window of `record_call` (the first `CALL_WINDOW` budget units after a budget reset), and as `Flow::Record`, so the T0 handler keeps no frame. A monomorphic StoreKeyed hit records nothing: its shape holds the kind, and the miss that installed the shape recorded it. So the set holds every kind a miss saw and the kinds of the sampled hits; a kind that only hits outside every window see is missing, as an unsampled call target is. Recording at every hit cost about 3% (t0-prop), 10% (crypto) and 13% (navier-stokes) more instructions than no hit record (M5-P1 BENCH-SET, Mac a64). No IC hit reads these bits, so `ics_only` (no record hooks, MODES-D19) hits the same and leaves the set empty. Schema 1 exports none of them (`facts_export.rs` `keyed` reads the KeySet bits only); the month-6 bump decides (F-R5). Tests `tests::elements::keyed_slots_record_the_kinds_they_saw`, `tests::elements::the_store_hit_keeps_the_kind`. [2026-11-04, M6-P3 B1W; FREEZE-M1 B1; W19C-7, M6-PLAN-D4:] The rule, frozen with "fact meanings": the bit of elements kind value k is bit 10 + k, append-only with the H18 list "elements kind values (append-only)", so a new kind has its bit already and moves no IC_LAYOUT; the pair (`IC_LAYOUT_VERSION`, mask) = (4, `0xFFFF << 10`) is pinned (`tests::elements::the_lattice_is_one_way`; `tests::layout6::the_kinds_set_pair_and_the_ran_bit`). The kind values are now in `elements::kind` under OBJECT_LAYOUT 6 (the text above says 5). Since FACT_SCHEMA 2 the export holds the set: `KeyedFacts.kinds` is the u16 `ElementsKindSet` (bit i = value i, an open list) and the bits 4-9 are `KeyedFacts.hint`, so "Schema 1 exports none of them" above is the schema 1 text.

**The keyed IC state is live only for StoreKeyed (MC-w15-1; `crates/ep9_vm/src/keyed_ic.rs`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs` `keyed`).** Only the StoreKeyed fast case (a present element of an Array) writes the key half of a keyed slot; the LoadKeyed, DefineKeyedOwn, HasKeyed and StoreInArrayLiteral bodies always go cold and leave it alone. So at those kinds, and at a StoreKeyed site that never took the fast case (an append, say), `Uninitialized` means "no state", not "never ran" (MODES-D17's "ran" bit is the only "never ran" signal), and a keyed `Monomorphic` (one receiver shape) exports no case: schema 1 has no element access to describe. The `ep9_facts` `IcState` and `KeyedFacts` docs say the same; test `keyed_ic_state_is_live_only_for_store_keyed_present_elements` in `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/facts_export.rs` pins it. [2026-11-04, M6-P3 B1W; FREEZE-M1 B1; plan v11 input #19, M5-P4-ORCH-9 (1):] An `ics_only` export holds no StoreKeyed IC state either: it leaves out every keyed kind (LoadKeyed, StoreKeyed, DefineKeyedOwn, HasKeyed, StoreInArrayLiteral; `facts_export.rs` `keep`), although the StoreKeyed fast case still writes the key half there. That is on purpose: a keyed fact is the record bits plus the key half, and with the record bits 0 the record would read "no key seen", a false fact; leaving the kind out is the safe form (F-R9: no reader gets a wrong fact).

**An empty BinaryOp or CompareOp slot means "int32 or never ran" (F-R9, MODES-D10; `crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs:72-92`).** T0 writes nothing in the int32 case (H16-Q2 (1)), and the exporter adds the INT32 bit to every record of the kind in a function that has a vector (`crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs:470-475`). A slot starts as int32 0, so a record whose only bit is INT32 cannot tell a cold site from an int32 site. AOT reads it that way; the H32 site budget bounds the cost when the site later sees other values. Two changes are decided:
1. MODES-D15: the one month-6 schema bump adds a per-site "ran" bit, written only inside the sampled window (no T0 cost outside it). AOT then compiles a never-ran site as generic and out of line, not as an int32 guard. [2026-11-04, M6-P3 B1W:] FACT_SCHEMA 2 has the field (`RecordFlags::RAN`); no T0 code writes its source yet ("Declared, no writer" below).
2. MODES-D16: the month-5 PROFILE step makes T0 record an int32 `+`, `-`, `Inc` or `Dec` that overflows (today the overflow stays implicit, H16-Q2 (1)). The write sits on the overflow path only, so the hit path does not change. Until then no fact backs an overflow guard of these four operations. [Built 2026-10-28, M5-P1 PROFILE5: T0 records `BinaryHint::INT32_OVERFLOW` ("int32 operands and a result outside int32", the bit `*`, `/` and `>>>` already write; not NUMBER, MC-w14-19) when an int32 `+` or `-` (`Add`, `Sub`, `AddSmi`, `SubSmi`, also in their `Wide` and `ExtraWide` forms) or an int32 `Inc` or `Dec` gives a result outside int32. The fast body returns the record from the branch the checked operation takes on overflow (`binop_fast`, `Inc` and `Dec` in `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/ops.rs`; `profile::INT32_OVERFLOW`), so the int32 result that fits tests nothing more, and `cargo xtask disasm-check --all-targets` keeps the handler and frame lists of the base on 5 targets. This replaces two sentences of the H16-Q2 cell (1): "An int32 `+`/`-` that overflows stays implicit" and "A unary op records no overflow"; `Negate` still records no overflow (not a D16 operation). So a BinaryOp record of these four operations without `INT32_OVERFLOW` now means "no overflow seen, or never ran", and a consumer may use it as the basis of their overflow guard (Spike C still treats that guard as unbacked). No slot kind, schema or encoding change (`FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1). Tests: `tests::profile` (one per arm: `an_int32_add_that_overflows_records_int32_overflow` and the `sub`, `inc` and `dec` forms; `d16_results_that_fit_and_negate_stay_implicit`; binary cases 1 and 13 now expect `INT32_OVERFLOW`).]

**Beside the schema (doc 13 C-06; F-R1, F-R4, §4.1).** [2026-11-04, M6-P3 B1W; FREEZE-M1 B1] Facts travel beside the CompiledUnit, never inside it: a unit's hints section stays empty, and the container `facts` section is their home (H02, the C-12 note; F-R1). The L2 side tables (function records, per-case hit counts, function digests, the run count, class records, tier hints) live in their container, each with its own version, so adding an L2 table moves no `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` (F-R4). The L2 tier-hint shape is per function: a priority byte with a "runs once" value, a stability tri-state (unknown, stable, unstable) and exit counts per class (doc 13 §4.1, O3); its format is decided at A3. The one month-6 bump (FACT_SCHEMA 2) added no VERIFIED field: VERIFIED facts are never FactSnapshot records (H30).

**Declared, no writer (MODES-CHECK MC-w24-5, MC-w25-7; M6-ORCH-26).** [2026-11-04, M6-P3 B1W; FREEZE-M1 B1] FACT_SCHEMA 2 has fields that no code writes at M1 exit, so no record holds them and a reader must not wait for them: `RecordFlags::RAN` (its LoadKeyed source, bit 26 `profile::keyed::RAN`, has no writer; [2026-11-05, M6-P4 fixer (MODES-CHECK-w27 MC-w27-3):] its BinaryOp and CompareOp source is bit 26 of their value half too, declared in IC_LAYOUT 4 (the "feedback core" part), with no writer and no reader: the exporter reads the hint bits as `u8` (BinaryOp) and `u16` (CompareOp), so a set bit 26 changes no record until the exporter maps it to `RecordFlags::RAN`; that writer and that map move no IC_LAYOUT, because the bit is declared here and no reader built today reads it); `RecordFlags::EXITED` (no VM code records a deopt exit per site; it comes with a recompile-on-exit path, A2); `ObjectKind::TypedArray` (no TypedArray kind exists; it comes with its lane); the Access tags getter and setter (the IC_HANDLER kinds 9-11 export as `generic` through M1, MODES-CHECK MC-w25-4; a typed writer needs FACT_SCHEMA 3); `Access::StringLength` (the STRING_LENGTH handler comes only under `ic::KEY_STRING`, and the exporter leaves out every KEY_STRING case, `facts_export.rs` `case`); `Access::Element`: a keyed record has no cases (`KeyedFacts.property.cases` is always empty, `facts_export.rs` `keyed`; MC-w15-1); `PropertyFacts.values`, `GlobalFacts.values` and `CallFacts.result`: T0 records no value types and no call result types (H16-Q2), so they are always empty; `BindingSet::MISSING` (`global` and `slow_global` write lexical, data-property and accessor-property only); the `ForInHint` bits `enum-cache-keys` and `enum-cache-keys-and-indices` (v0 has no enum cache: `profile::for_in_bits` always gives `any`); the `polymorphic` IC state of a Call or keyed record (a Call slot keeps one callee or the hole, and a keyed slot one shape or the hole: `facts_export.rs` `callees` and `keyed` give uninitialized, monomorphic or megamorphic only); `FieldRep::Unset` (a new field gets the representation of its first value, or `any`: `shape::add_with`, so no shape holds `unset`); and `LiteralFacts.elements` `dictionary` (dictionary is not a birth kind: an array-literal site keeps `holey-elements` at most, `elements.rs` `generalize_site`). [2026-11-04, M6-P3 Codex review r1 D4: the six items after getter and setter added. M6-P3 Codex review r2 X2: the items from the `ForInHint` bits to `LiteralFacts.elements` `dictionary` added.] [2026-11-05, spec-temporal B3: the `bigint` bits of BinaryHint, CompareHint and the TypeOf TypeSet left this list: `profile.rs` writes them for a BigInt operand (`binary_operand`, `compare_operand`, `type_of`).] The confidence byte is absent from a RUNTIME snapshot by rule; its production writers are the PROFILED and HINTED writers of A3 and A4 (H16-Q5b). Every other item of a record has a writer that fires. `tests::facts_export::the_fields_declared_with_no_writer_have_none` checks each item of this list (no code writes it; a snapshot holds none of it). `every_field_that_claims_a_writer_is_seen` (`crates/ep9_builtins/tests/facts_export.rs`) is the other half of the MC-w24-5 check: it makes the items of a record from the schema (each slot kind; each byte-enum value, bitset bit and tag at each place, from `ALL`, `BITS` and `TAGS`; each count and value-type field that is not 0), runs richards, `KINDS` and `WRITERS` with full feedback (and `DEAD_KEY` with a full GC before its export), and fails with the name of each item that is not in this list and that no record holds, and of each item of this list that a record holds.

## Freeze table

README §3.4 form (plan v11 §5.6, FREEZE-M1 B1; doc 13 C-25, C-24, C-06, §5.4; M6-PLAN-D24). C-25 names four parts: the tuning part (gated), the safety rules, the append-only lists and the fact meanings. "feedback core" holds every other row, so that each row of this ADR is in one part (the Frozen check reads parts, not rows). A part name holds no `,` and no ` and `, because the Frozen check splits the Status parenthesis there (`tools/xtask/src/exit/freeze.rs`). The one Frozen step (M6-P4) writes `Frozen (feedback core, safety rules, fact meanings; <date>, M1 exit)`.

| Part | What it holds | Freeze point | After the freeze point | Check |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| feedback core | Every row of this ADR except the parts below: the plan row (Decision); the slot kinds, bytes 0-20 (K1; H2 ABI bytes); the runtime record (K2), the IC states (K3), the handler forms (K4, K9: the smi kind in bits 0-3 and the byte offset in bits 4-31, with the K9 width rule; K10: the IC_HANDLER and POLY_IC cells), validity cells (K5), the vector (K6), weak IC data (K7), K11-K18 as built; the IC_LAYOUT 4 value half of a keyed slot (bits 0-9 the key and receiver bits, bits 10-25 the kinds set, bit 26 the LoadKeyed "ran" bit, bits 27-30 reserved); [2026-11-05, M6-P4 fixer (MODES-CHECK-w27 MC-w27-3):] the IC_LAYOUT 4 value half of a BinaryOp and a CompareOp slot (the hint bits as built: BinaryOp bits 0-6, CompareOp bits 0-9; bit 26 the MODES-D15 "ran" bit, declared, no writer; the other bits up to bit 30 reserved); the FactSnapshot schema as FACT_SCHEMA 2 (`schema.txt`, `schema-history.txt` line 2) with its open-list rule (an append to an open list is a fill: the names are outside the fingerprint; [2026-11-05, M6-P4 fixer (B-CHECK F8):] a new name on a name line of `schema.txt` goes with the append-only part of its VM values, and that append re-blesses `schema.txt` (`schema_text_matches_the_golden_file`) as part of its fill: `FieldRep` with H17 "representation values (append-only)", `ShapeFlags` with H17 "shape flag bits (append-only)", `ElementsKind` and `ElementsKindSet` with H18 "elements kind values (append-only)"; the ElementsKind names are the H18 part "FactSnapshot kind list"); the CacheIR op-list form (`ops_of`: guards first, then one action; refs, not pointers) and the list of each existing handler kind; the H16-Q1, Q4 and Q5 fills; and every Rules row | M1 exit | Frozen-part rules (README §3.2) | the Rules rows (`ep9_bytecode` `tests/abi.rs`; `ep9_facts` tests with `schema_history_pins_the_schema`; `tests::ic`, `tests::feedback`, `tests::validity`; T262 and T262-EAGER give the same sets); `tests::ic_ops` (one test per handler kind and site operation); `tests::layout6::the_kinds_set_pair_and_the_ran_bit`; `tests::layout3::ic_handler_kind_lists_state_their_width` |
| safety rules | C-25 (2): no stub cache entry outlives a cell it names: the table is emptied at every collection, minor ones included (H16-Q3, the Codex D2-1 note); a table kept across a minor GC needs a minor-GC weak filter first. The instanceof hit walk: at most one group of walk steps goes uncharged, and a walk that uses up the interrupt budget misses after at most one group past it, so the generic walk polls (H16-Q3, MC-w16-5) | M1 exit | Frozen-part rules (README §3.2) | `tests::stub_cache::the_stub_cache_is_empty_after_every_gc`, `tests::stub_cache::an_entry_keeps_nothing_alive`; `tests::instance_ic::a_termination_stops_the_hit_walk_of_a_warm_site_on_a_long_chain` |
| fact meanings | C-25 (4) and C-06: the section "Fact meanings" (the unit of each count, F-R7; a call key is never written out, F-R8; an empty BinaryOp or CompareOp slot means "int32 or never ran", F-R9; the keyed kinds-set rule, bit 10 + k for kind value k; the keyed IC state is live only for StoreKeyed; an `ics_only` export leaves out the record kinds); the MODES-D16 overflow record, the MODES-D17 "ran" sites and the MODES-D18 rule; the H16-Q2 meanings (implicit int32, the `ics_only` condition, C-27); "Beside the schema" (F-R1, F-R4; no VERIFIED field). [2026-11-05, M6-P4 fixer (MODES-CHECK-w27 MC-w27-2):] The paragraph "Declared, no writer" is not in this part: it is the part "declared fields (shrink-only)" | M1 exit | Frozen-part rules (README §3.2) | `tests::facts_export`; `ep9_builtins` `tests/facts_export.rs`; `tests::profile`; `tests::elements::keyed_slots_record_the_kinds_they_saw`; `ep9_builtins` `tests/presets_object_model.rs` (`an_ics_only_export_has_no_record_kind`) |

A new op, type or handler kind is a fill when its first reader is built; an existing one never changes its meaning; a reader that meets an unknown one treats the case as a miss (generic path; `ops_of` gives `[Generic]` for a kind it does not know) [2026-11-05, M6-P4 fixer (M6-ORCH-88 (3)):] The `FeedbackSlotKind` values join this part (`crates/ep9_bytecode/src/feedback.rs`; 21 kinds, bytes 0-20 at CF; the H2 side is H2 "opcode and section appends (append-only)", with its `ABI_VERSION` bump): a new slot kind at the next free byte is a fill. Part of its fill, not a change of "feedback core": its runtime record in the K2 form; its fact record, its `Payload::of` arm (`ep9_facts` `Payload::of` is a match with no wildcard arm, so a new kind does not compile until it has one); and the `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump with the new kind line of `schema.txt` and the new line of `schema-history.txt` (`schema_text_lists_every_kind`; `codec.rs` refuses a kind byte it does not know). The 21 kinds at CF stay in "feedback core" (K1): a renumber or a change of one of them is a rewrite [2026-11-05, M6-P5 orchestrator (MODES-CHECK-w28 MC-w28-2; M6-ORCH-99, M6-ORCH-100):] The appends to the closed lists of the FactSnapshot schema join this part too. A FACT_SCHEMA bump whose only change is an append to a closed list of `schema.txt` (a new enum value, tag or bitset bit at the next free place, or a new field at the end of a payload) is a fill of this part. A closed list is a list of `schema.txt` outside its "open lists" (only `ElementsKind`, `FieldRep`, `ShapeFlags` and `ElementsKindSet` are open): the Access, ProtoRef, KeyRef and FunctionRef tags, the enums and the bitsets; `codec.rs` refuses a value of a closed list that it does not know. Part of its fill, not a change of "feedback core": the `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump, the new line of `schema-history.txt` and the re-blessed `schema.txt` (`schema_text_matches_the_golden_file`, `schema_history_pins_the_schema`); the new value or field in the `ep9_facts` test sample, with its re-blessed `facts-v0.txt` and `facts-v0.hex` (`sample_covers_every_kind_tag_and_enum_value`, `sample_bytes_match_the_golden_file`, `sample_dump_matches_the_golden_file`; each bump changes the version line of both); and a new arm in each test match over the list (for Access: `ep9_vm` `tests::facts_export`, `ep9_builtins` `tests/facts_export.rs`). An append is a fill only when every existing Rules check, test and golden file keeps its result (README §3.3, fill: "Every existing Rules check, test and golden file keeps its result"); this holds for the slot-kind appends of the M6-ORCH-88 (3) note above too, with one named part of their fill: by the H02:175 rule (H2 part "opcode and section appends (append-only)"), the count pin 21 of `ep9_facts` `schema_text_lists_every_kind` (`tests/schema.rs:122`) and of `ep9_vm` `tests::layout4::ic_layout_2_constants` moves with a slot-kind append as part of its fill. At CF it does not hold yet: some tests pin the present last value, the present count or a fixed entry size, or probe the next free value or bit (against README §3.4). Examples in `ep9_facts`: `tag_tables_match_the_tags` (`tests/schema.rs:245` to `:251`; Access tags 10 and 11 with `Generic` at 9 fail it); `header_errors`, `record_errors` and `bitsets_merge_by_or` (`tests/encoding.rs:534`, `:607`, `:613`, `:1039` probe `FactSource` 3, `TypeSet` bit 12 and `RecordFlags` bit 2); `shapes_that_fold_into_one_merge_their_cases` (`tests/encoding.rs:336` and `:337` write at fixed offsets of the 17-byte `ShapeDesc` entry of the test shape `bare()`). The list is not closed: the rule is the README sentence, not this list. The test change that makes these tests append-safe (each tag byte pinned, the top value or bit probed, entry sizes read from the bytes, no count pin), with no result change at CF, is routed to M2 (MC-P5-FACTTOP). Until it lands, an append that changes a test result is a rewrite of "feedback core"; the review of the first append (M6-ORCH-100) runs every `ep9_facts` test and both `facts_export` tests. An existing value or field never changes its byte, place or meaning; a renumber, or a change of an existing value or field, is a rewrite of "feedback core". A reader of the old version drops the snapshot (H35:56; `codec.rs` refuses another `schema_version`). First planned user: the typed getter and setter facts, as new Access tags 10 and 11 with the field index (M6-ORCH-100; tags 0-9 are used at CF); the tags 3 (getter) and 4 (setter) do not change (H16:224; DL M6-P1-MC-W25-4). `ShapeFlags` is a u8, so it holds bits 0-7 only: a shape flag that needs a snapshot bit past bit 7 takes a new field at the end of `ShapeDesc` (an append, so a fill of this part), never a wider `ShapeFlags`. This note does not change a Frozen part of another ADR: a value that such a part names keeps the rule of that part (for example, a new `FactSource` value is also a change of the provenance set of H30 "Model", H30:106) | None | An append is a fill (README §3.4 "Append-only parts"); an existing value never changes; G8 never counts an append | `tests::layout3::ic_handler_kind_lists_state_their_width` (the smi value 15, the probe at the top of the range, and 0 are no kind; 7 and 8 are the free pair K9 keeps for the next checked representation, and no negative test probes them); `tests::ic_ops::every_list_is_guards_then_one_action`, `tests::ic_ops::handler_parts_reads_each_handler_kind_as_ic_does`, `tests::ic_ops::accessor_and_dictionary_holder_handlers_have_their_lists` (the IC_HANDLER probe `i32::MAX`, the top of the int32 kind range, gives `[Generic]`; 12, the next free kind, is not probed); `tests::stub_cache::a_load_entry_is_never_used_for_a_store_or_a_define` |

## Reopens if

- SP-Facts misses its gate (09 §4).
- M1 needs a slot kind before the freeze: one bump, by the steward (bytecode ABI and fact schema together).
- Per-function feedback turns sites polymorphic much more often than V8 (H16-Q1).

| 2026-10-29 | rewrite (proposed; pre-freeze churn; Accepted path) | The type-feedback mode `ics_only` takes effect (MODES-D11, MODES-D19; M5-P3 PROFILE6, plan v10 §5.3; orchestrator M5-P3-ORCH-4: the D11 pick is a second T0 handler table). An isolate whose `ExecOptions` feedback mode is `ics_only` (the default of the jitless preset, so of every isolate of a build without the `jit` feature) runs no record of `profile.rs`: its T0 table is the second table of its expansion (`interp::Dispatch::table`, chosen once per `Cx` by `Isolate::new_with`), whose fast handlers continue at the next instruction instead of the `Flow::Record`, `RecordPair` and `JumpRecordCall` records; the cold handlers are shared with the `full` table, and their record hooks (and the polling `JumpLoop` count) read the mode (`profile::records`) and write nothing; the match loop reads the mode once per entry and the prefixed step per record. Every IC write and every object-model input (allocation sites with the ALLOC_SITE cell, clone handlers, slack, field representation, elements kinds) is the same as under `full`. So the "What T0 records" rules and the Fact meanings hold for `full` isolates (the jit preset, a training run, `.feedback(Feedback::FULL)`); under `ics_only` every slot that only `profile.rs` writes keeps its first state (undefined, int32 0) and a keyed slot keeps its record bits 0, and the exporter gives no record facts for it [2026-11-01, M5-P4 integration fixer; MODES-CHECK-w21 MC-w21-1, M5-P3-ORCH-14: true from then on; until then the exporter wrote such a slot's first state, so a BinaryOp or CompareOp site of an `ics_only` export had the implicit INT32 bit only, "int32 or never ran" (F-R9). The rewrite row of 2026-11-01 names the kinds left out]. Kind: rewrite, because the records of a jitless isolate change (tests that read them now ask for `full`). Tests: `tests::profile` (each record test also runs its program under `ics_only` and checks that no record is written and every IC slot ends as under `full`; the live-shape vector-axis test), `ep9_builtins` `tests/presets_object_model.rs`.
