(2) `ShapeDesc` holds the shape's elements kind (a byte, value + 1, 0 = none) and, in each key's attrs byte, the field hint of that key: `FieldRep` in bits 4-6 (the H17 representation values: unset, int32, double, heap, any) and the const bit 7 (`ShapeDesc::fields`; M6-PLAN-D8). (3) `KeyedFacts` holds `hint` (u8 `KeyedHint`: array, object, string, primitive, out of bounds, hole, the keyed slot bits 4-9) and `kinds` (u16 `ElementsKindSet`, the IC_LAYOUT 4 kinds set, bits 10-25; M6-PLAN-D4). (4) `ElementsKind` takes the VM kind values (H18 "FactSnapshot kind list"); every elements-kind field, `Access::Element` too, is a byte value + 1. (5) `ObjectKind` appends arguments 7 and typed-array 8 (typed-array declared, no writer: the VM has no typed array kind). (6) Four open lists, `ElementsKind`, `FieldRep`, `ShapeFlags` and `ElementsKindSet`: a value or bit a reader does not name reads as none (elements kind), any (rep) or is dropped (bits), for that field only, never a refusal of the snapshot (MC-w23-8); two shapes that fold into one merge their cases. Their names are a schema-text section that the fingerprint leaves out (`schema_format_text`), so an append is a fill. (7) The version probes read `u32::MAX`, never the next value. First planned user: the typed getter and setter facts as new Access tags 10 and 11 with the field index (M6-ORCH-100; tags 3 and 4 do not change; H16:224, DL M6-P1-MC-W25-4). `ShapeFlags` is a u8 (bits 0-7 only): a snapshot bit past bit 7 takes a new field at the end of `ShapeDesc` (an append, so a fill), never a wider `ShapeFlags`; a value that a Frozen part of another ADR names keeps the rule of that part (a new `FactSource` value is also a change of H30 "Model", H30:106). (2) A dated note at the end of the "What it holds" cell of "declared fields (shrink-only)" (H16:237): such a bump is a fill of "IC lists (append-only)" by the (1) note; a slot-kind append with its bump stays a fill by the M6-ORCH-88 (3) note; any other FACT_SCHEMA bump stays a "feedback core" change. Kind: the notes move the closed-list appends out of the Frozen part "feedback core" to a part with no freeze point, and they change the H16:237 rule of the part a bump belongs to, so the part accepts by fill what it took only by rewrite before. That is not a clarify, and not a fill (no Open question or deferred part held these appends), so it is a rewrite (README §3.3: "Anything else."), as item (5) of the M6-P4 fixer row of 2026-11-05 was for the slot kinds before the freeze. Between the freeze and the M1 exit tag: not G8; the M1 report names it (README §3.3, the 2026-11-02 note; the form of M6-P2-H20-RW, M6-ORCH-37). No check, test or golden file changes result, and no code changes (plan v11 rule 43). Data: the code at HEAD (`schema.txt`: Access tags 0-9, so 10 and 11 are the next free tags; the open lists; `ShapeFlags u8`. `codec.rs`: the refusals of an unknown `FactSource`, `RecordFlags` bit, `TypeSet` bit, `ObjectKind` and Access tag, and of another `schema_version`. `schema-history.txt`: the lines of FACT_SCHEMA 1 and 2. `ep9_facts` `tests/schema.rs:245` to `:251` and `tests/encoding.rs:534`, `:607`, `:613`, `:1039`, `:336`, `:337`, `tests/schema.rs:122`, `ep9_vm` `tests::layout4`: the last-tag asserts, the next-free probes, the fixed `ShapeDesc` offsets and the slot-kind count pins). The orchestrator wrote this row and the notes, so a second reviewer agent from another plan role approves (README §3.2). the PROTO_LOAD, MISSING and ADD rows) makes compiled code depend on a VALIDITY_CELL, but H17's validity row, H31-Q1 and doc 13 §3.5 (C-01, C-02, MC-w13-11) say compiled code depends on a ProtoRef descriptor, never on the cell: a CellRef bound to the cell fails for good after one invalidation, and one read through the IC slot sees the new valid cell of the relearned case while LoadHolderField still names the old holder (in the probe it would return 1; node prints 2); fix: `GuardProtoValidity(ProtoRef)` = the handler's cell in T0 and a Dynamic FuseRef in compiled and unit code. **R-CI-2 (b, medium):** for an out-of-line ADD, T0 stores only into room the SLOTS cell has, else it misses (`ic.rs:458-462`); ops_of(ADD) has no such guard, so a reader that emits AddField as a plain store writes past the SLOTS cell when the add needs growth; fix: a `GuardSlotsRoom(obj, location)` guard (a miss takes the generic path) in the ADD row. **R-CI-3 (b, low):** 'the H30 ref nodes (ShapeRef, CellRef, RootRef)' (H16:145), but the ops also use FunctionRef, a holder ref and a record ref, and FunctionRef is not one of the five H30 ref nodes (H30:61); fix: name the ref node or relocation that each one lowers to. **R-CI-4 (b, low):** T0 keys ARRAY_LENGTH on the array's shape (`ic.rs:43`), so GuardKind is a widening (sound: an ARRAY's `length` is the int32 at +24), which the text should say; the lexical-binding global case (a FIELD smi handler keyed on the lexical record) misses on the TDZ hole in T0 (`ic.rs:500`, `:530`), and ops_of(FIELD_*) does not carry that check; fix: a `GuardNotHole` op for that case. **R-CI-5 (a, low):** 'T0 never runs an op list' (H16:143) goes further than V9-D9; after batch 1 a T0 or GenInterp form that runs lists would be a rewrite; fix: 'T0 keeps its handlers; a later list reader in T0 or GenInterp is a fill'. (1) Flush rule (Codex D2-1): the H16-Q3 fixer C note says the table is emptied at every collection, minor ones included, and that a table kept across a minor GC first needs a minor-GC weak filter of every cell an entry names. Code: `StubCache::new` registers `flush` as a weak source of the heap (`stub_cache.rs`, no change since 2e016d0); `collect_scanning` (heap.rs) calls `roots.visit_weak` for a minor and a major GC alike; `flush` sets every key to 0 when an entry went in since the last GC. The reason holds: `ic::record` makes the handler and then puts a megamorphic case in the table (`stub_insert`), so a new IC_HANDLER can be named by the table only, and `ic::load_with` reads that cell. `tests::stub_cache` (9, with `the_stub_cache_is_empty_after_every_gc`) passes in debug and `--release`. Plan: v9 §5.3 IC2 says flushed at every GC (the H16-Q3 default); the §6.7 MC-w15-7a item asked for 'at least at every major GC'; the orchestrator's D2-1 input (fixC-inputs) chose to keep 'flush at every collection', which the note does, so the plan's -7a wording is superseded ((c), a plan note for the plan owner). (2) InstanceOf hit budget (B1-1; the text as 319f303 left it): `instance_ic::hit` walks in groups of `WALK_STEPS` (8): the inner loop returns TRUE (a match), FALSE (the end of the chain) or a miss (a proxy) with no unit, and only after a full group does `*budget -= WALK_STEPS as i64` run, with a miss when the budget is at or below 0. So a walk of 8 steps or fewer takes no unit, the last 1 to 8 steps of a longer walk take none, a walk that uses up a budget of 1 or more runs at most 7 steps past it, and one that starts at 0 or below runs 8 steps (`call_bytecode` in call.rs takes its unit at line 496, after its `poll` at line 470, so a callee can start at 0). A miss goes to the cold path: `property::instance_of` reaches `prototype_walk_step`, which takes a unit and polls at 0 or below at its first step. The note's 'up to 8 steps past it' covers both cases. Author evidence: fixer C measured a check per step at +7.2% and per 8 steps at +1.2% instructions (wb-inst-mono +1.17% against 930945e; BENCH-SET within 0.2%); verifier C repeated the numbers, and its mutation M8 (no budget in the hit walk) fails `a_termination_stops_the_hit_walk_of_a_warm_site_on_a_long_chain`, which passes at 5281c03 (`tests::instance_ic` 10, debug and `--release`). One left-over outside the ADR at 5281c03: the doc comment of that test said the hit walk 'takes one interrupt budget unit per step' (a code comment; records reviewer C report D1); fixed in 12ab1e5 (comment only, records check C F1). Kind rewrite (proposed; pre-freeze churn; Accepted path): agree: the flush rule rejects again what the 7a note accepted, so a decided statement changes; H16 freezes at M1 exit, so G8 does not count it; the hit-budget note adds to the IC2 text and changes no state, layout or fact. A probe (a temporary test, reverted) gives the LoadKeyed kinds set of `kinds_program` as 0x5c00 when the script starts at `BUDGET` and also at `BUDGET + 100`, and 0x800 at `BUDGET - 100`. So 'only in the sampled window of `record_call`' (this row and the Fact meanings paragraph) does not hold at 7c70de6. Fix: `if st.cx.budget > BUDGET { return 0; }` after `cold_path()` (cold path only), and a test at `BUDGET + 1`. (1) `interp::slow::interrupt` (`slow/mod.rs:133`) is the poll reset, and it sets `cx.budget = profile::budget_after_poll(rt)`: `BUDGET` plus the low 7 bits of a mixed state, so 0..127 units (`CALL_JITTER` 128 = 2 x `CALL_WINDOW`, a power of two by a const assert); the state is per isolate (`Runtime::call_window_jitter`) and starts from one fixed constant, so every isolate and process gets the same sequence; `JITTER_OFF` gives the fixed reset. T0 polls, the native entries (`native.rs` 347, 361, 841, 1116) and `Cx::poll_interrupt` (`abi/index.rs:95`) all reach that function. `record_call` records only for a budget in (`BUDGET` - 64, `BUDGET`]: the lower bound on the hot path, the upper bound after `cold_path()`; `record_call_pushed` tests the upper bound too. (2) `tests::profile` 24 pass; `cargo test -p ep9_builtins --test facts_export` 7 pass with `a_periodic_call_pattern_reaches_every_site_through_the_jittered_window`. (3) Kind test applied (README §3.3 rewrite: 'a value that code, data or a file format depends on changes'; a test is replaced, so an existing test result changes): rewrite. Author: rewrite (proposed). Confirmed. Open (b): (i) the CI step 'spike-native tests (ep9_vm)' (`ci.yml:240-242`, `cargo test -p ep9_vm --features spike-native --locked native`) fails 2 tests at 7c70de6: `tests::native::a_native_callee_whose_frame_does_not_fit_takes_one_unit` and `a_native_callee_whose_frame_fits_takes_one_unit` ('AotBaseline Undefined budget 1', left -38, right 4). They count units across a poll as `budget + (BUDGET - left)`, so the jitter adds its j units. With `call_window_jitter` set to `JITTER_OFF` in `run_native_overflow` (a temporary edit, reverted) both pass. `--features jit,spike-native --lib`: 638 pass, the same 2 fail. Fix: set `JITTER_OFF` there, as the `interp/ops.rs` budget tests do. (ii) `keyed_hit_kind` has no upper bound, so keyed hits record in the jitter units (the ELEM1 row above, Open (b)) | branch m1m5/p1-merge at 7c70de6, mac a64; logs scratchpad m1m5/p1/review (rec-vm.log, rec-facts-export.log, rec-ci-native.log, rec-vm-jit-native.log, rec-native-jitteroff-probe.log) |
